{\"noOutputExpected\": false}\n\n========================================================================================================================\n\nSTEP 14\nPATTERN multi_anim_sess\n1→# Preprocessing guide\n     2→\n     3→This is meant to serve as a reference for how data were preprocessed from the raw imaging/VR files to the several levels of data structures used for analysis.\n     4→\n     5→All file paths are written as if we are in the current repo, i.e. the pip installable modules will be listed as `./src/reward_relative`  \\\n     6→If I say \"module\" I mean to look for a .py file in `./src/reward_relative`  \\\n     7→\n     8→### Directory structure\n     9→\n    10→* data_root: e.g. `/data/`\n    11→    * `2P_Data` (sometimes omitted locally)\n    12→        * animal\n    13→            * date: dd_mm_yyyy\n    14→                * scene: e.g. Env1_LocationA\n    15→                    * If you copied the scanbox files locally, they would be here as .sbx and .mat alongside the below directory\n    16→                    * session + scan directory: e.g. Env1_LocationA_002_001; session (index of Unity VR file) = 002, scan number (index of scan in Scanbox) = 001\n    17→                        * suite2p\n    18→                            * plane0 (plane1, combined, etc if multiplane)\n    19→    * `VR_Data`\n    20→        * animal\n    21→            * date: dd_mm_yyy\n    22→                * SQLite files: scene_session.sqlite, e.g. Env1_LocationA_2.sqlite\n    23→\n    24→### Order of operations:\n    25→\n    26→0. Update `./src/reward_relative/sessions_dict.py` with the metadata from each recording session, with one dictionary entry per animal in either `single_plane` or `multi_plane`\n    27→    1. Include date, scene name, session, scan number, experiment day (1-indexed)\n    28→1. Extract binaries, run suite2p to do motion correction and find ROIs\n    29→    1. In `./notebooks/suite2p_notebooks`, duplicate an existing notebook and rename for the current animal\n    30→    2. Manually edit with the sessions you want to process\n    31→    3. Manually edit any suite2p parameters you want to change (for example, if you think the expression was weak, you could lower threshold_scaling from 1 to 0.6-0.8)\n    32→    4. For Scanbox data, running the main notebook cell will convert the .sbx files to .h5 and then run suite2p\n    33→2. Curate cells in the suite2p GUI\n    34→    1. `iscell.npy` is automatically updated and saved during curation\n    35→    2. Be sure to backup curated data so you don't lose all that work!\n    36→3. Copy VR_Data to the local machine with rclone or similar\n    37→4. Make `sess` class which aligns imaging data to Unity VR data.\n    38→    1. The code for the class object itself is in a separate repo, `TwoPUtils` \n    39→    2. The function that actually interpolates/aligns VR data to the slower imaging frame rate is `vr_align_to_2P` in `TwoPUtils/preprocessing.py`\n    40→    3. Use `./notebooks/make_session_pkl.ipynb` to make sess for each animal and list of experiment days that you want.\n    41→    4. `sess` pickles will be saved in `/data_root/sess/`\n    42→    5. Note `sess` does not contain deltaF/F -- this happens next, because it is more customizable. The idea is that you should only ever have to make the `sess` class once.\n    43→5. Make `multi_anim_sess` dictionary to collect and post-process sess across animals.\n    44→    1. Use `./notebooks/make_multi_anim_sess.ipynb`\n    45→    2. The core function `multi_anim_sess` in the `utilities` module runs functions to compute deltaF/F (dFF), calculates place cells from shuffles, and adds details like a trial set dictionary.\n    46→        1. The function that actually computes dFF is `dff` in the `preprocessing` module\n    47→    2. All of these are collected with the sess data for multiple animals on a single day, and stored in a dictionary where each top entry is an animal name. \n    48→    3. The notebook then saves this `multi_anim_sess` as a pickle so that we can work off a constant set of place cell IDs.\n    49→        1. TO-DO to free up disk space: save the sess pickle path as a pointer rather than re-saving the whole class inside multi_anim_sess\n    50→6. Align ROIs across days (can happen at any time after making sess)\n    51→    1. Use `./notebooks/Run_ROI_Aligner.ipynb`\n    52→    2. Saves matched ROI pair indices in `/data_root/sess/<animal>/roi_aligned_results.pkl`\n    53→7. For most analyses in Sosa, Plitt, Giocomo 2024, it is then necessary to create a `dayData` class for each experiment day, across animals.\n    54→    1. Use `./notebooks/make_multiDayData_class.ipynb`. `./reward_relative/dayData` has its own module with some helper functions. This `dayData` class contains computationally-intensive results like identifying reward-relative cells compared to a trial-by-trial shuffle of their activity.\n    55→8. Run remaining analyses and plot figures in `./notebooks` (more notebook figures will be posted as they are cleaned up for readability and documented).\n    56→\n    57→\n\n[metadata] {\"type\": \"text\", \"file\": {\"filePath\": \"/app/code/docs/preprocessing_guide.md\", \"content\": \"# Preprocessing guide\\n\\nThis is meant to serve as a reference for how data were preprocessed from the raw imaging/VR files to the several levels of data structures used for analysis.\\n\\nAll file paths are written as if we are in the current repo, i.e. the pip installable modules will be listed as `./src/reward_relative`  \\\\\\nIf I say \\\"module\\\" I mean to look for a .py file in `./src/reward_relative`  \\\\\\n\\n### Directory structure\\n\\n* data_root: e.g. `/data/`\\n    * `2P_Data` (sometimes omitted locally)\\n        * animal\\n            * date: dd_mm_yyyy\\n                * scene: e.g. Env1_LocationA\\n                    * If you copied the scanbox files locally, they would be here as .sbx and .mat alongside the below directory\\n                    * session + scan directory: e.g. Env1_LocationA_002_001; session (index of Unity VR file) = 002, scan number (index of scan in Scanbox) = 001\\n                        * suite2p\\n                            * plane0 (plane1, combined, etc if multiplane)\\n    * `VR_Data`\\n        * animal\\n            * date: dd_mm_yyy\\n                * SQLite files: scene_session.sqlite, e.g. Env1_LocationA_2.sqlite\\n\\n### Order of operations:\\n\\n0. Update `./src/reward_relative/sessions_dict.py` with the metadata from each recording session, with one dictionary entry per animal in either `single_plane` or `multi_plane`\\n    1. Include date, scene name, session, scan number, experiment day (1-indexed)\\n1. Extract binaries, run suite2p to do motion correction and find ROIs\\n    1. In `./notebooks/suite2p_notebooks`, duplicate an existing notebook and rename for the current animal\\n    2. Manually edit with the sessions you want to process\\n    3. Manually edit any suite2p parameters you want to change (for example, if you think the expression was weak, you could lower threshold_scaling from 1 to 0.6-0.8)\\n    4. For Scanbox data, running the main notebook cell will convert the .sbx files to .h5 and then run suite2p\\n2. Curate cells in the suite2p GUI\\n    1. `iscell.npy` is automatically updated and saved during curation\\n    2. Be sure to backup curated data so you don't lose all that work!\\n3. Copy VR_Data to the local machine with rclone or similar\\n4. Make `sess` class which aligns imaging data to Unity VR data.\\n    1. The code for the class object itself is in a separate repo, `TwoPUtils` \\n    2. The function that actually interpolates/aligns VR data to the slower imaging frame rate is `vr_align_to_2P` in `TwoPUtils/preprocessing.py`\\n    3. Use `./notebooks/make_session_pkl.ipynb` to make sess for each animal and list of experiment days that you want.\\n    4. `sess` pickles will be saved in `/data_root/sess/`\\n    5. Note `sess` does not contain deltaF/F -- this happens next, because it is more customizable. The idea is that you should only ever have to make the `sess` class once.\\n5. Make `multi_anim_sess` dictionary to collect and post-process sess across animals.\\n    1. Use `./notebooks/make_multi_anim_sess.ipynb`\\n    2. The core function `multi_anim_sess` in the `utilities` module runs functions to compute deltaF/F (dFF), calculates place cells from shuffles, and adds details like a trial set dictionary.\\n        1. The function that actually computes dFF is `dff` in the `preprocessing` module\\n    2. All of these are collected with the sess data for multiple animals on a single day, and stored in a dictionary where each top entry is an animal name. \\n    3. The notebook then saves this `multi_anim_sess` as a pickle so that we can work off a constant set of place cell IDs.\\n        1. TO-DO to free up disk space: save the sess pickle path as a pointer rather than re-saving the whole class inside multi_anim_sess\\n6. Align ROIs across days (can happen at any time after making sess)\\n    1. Use `./notebooks/Run_ROI_Aligner.ipynb`\\n    2. Saves matched ROI pair indices in `/data_root/sess/<animal>/roi_aligned_results.pkl`\\n7. For most analyses in Sosa, Plitt, Giocomo 2024, it is then necessary to create a `dayData` class for each experiment day, across animals.\\n    1. Use `./notebooks/make_multiDayData_class.ipynb`. `./reward_relative/dayData` has its own module with some helper functions. This `dayData` class contains computationally-intensive results like identifying reward-relative cells compared to a trial-by-trial shuffle of their activity.\\n8. Run remaining analyses and plot figures in `./notebooks` (more notebook figures will be posted as they are cleaned up for readability and documented).\\n\\n\", \"numLines\": 57, \"startLine\": 1, \"totalLines\": 57}}\n\n========================================================================================================================\n\nSTEP 15\nPATTERN multi_anim_sess\n1→# Extended documentation for data stored in multi_anim_sess\n     2→\n     3→As an example, let's take: multi_anim_sess file 2-3-4-6-7-10-11-12-13-14-15-17-18-19_expday3_speed2_perms100_maximin_events.pickle.\n     4→\n     5→This filename means:\n     6→\n     7→* it includes mice m2, m3, m4, m6, m7, m10, m11, m12, m12, m13, m14, m15, m17, m18, m19. 2,3,4,7,11,12,13,14,15,17,18,19 learned the reward zone switch task.\n     8→* it contains data from experiment day 3 (the 1st switch day)\n     9→* place cells were identified taking neural activity at running speeds >2 cm/s\n    10→* 100 permutations were used for place cell significance calculations\n    11→* the dF/F baseline was calculated using a `maximin` filter\n    12→* deconvolved calcium `events` were used to define place cells\n    13→\n    14→Each pickle file is a collection of dictionaries, with a key for each animal, plus a key for the `params` used to define place cells. Animals were originally named by indicator (i.e. GCAMP) and a number.\n    15→\n    16→If you call the entry for a given animal, say \"GCAMP4\", it is a dictionary with keys: \n    17→```\n    18→dict_keys(['animal', 'sess', 'pc masks set0', 'pc masks set1', 'SI set0', 'SI set1', 'p set0', 'p set1', 'pc perms set0', 'pc perms set1', 'SI perms set0', 'SI perms set1', 'isreward', 'morph', 'dream', 'rzone', 'rz label', 'trial dict'])\n    19→```\n    20→\n    21→* `pc masks setx` is the boolean indicator telling you which unit/cell is a place cell or not in each trial set, where True means p<0.05 that it's a place cell\n    22→    * set0 is the 30 trials before the reward switch, set1 is all the trials after the reward switch\n    23→    * if there was no reward switch that day, set0 is the first half, set1 is the second half\n    24→    * I typically define \"overall\" place cells to be cells with significant spatial information (p<0.05, True) in set0 OR set1\n    25→* `sess` is a class created by the TwoPUtils repo with the neural and behavioral data from the session\n    26→* `SI`, `p` for each trial set are the spatial information per cell and p-value compared to shuffle per cell. The `perms` or shuffle information were not saved on figshare to reduce filesize.\n    27→* `is_reward` refers to a 1 x N trials binary array of whether a given trial was rewarded or not (1=rewarded, 0=omission trial)\n    28→* `morph` is a 1 x N trials binary array of which environment was active (0=Env 1, 1=Env 2)\n    29→* `dream` is something unused from another experiment, ignore it (it will be all zeros)\n    30→* `rzone` is a 2 x N trials array of the reward zone start and end coordinates, in cm\n    31→* `rz label` is a 1 x N trials array with text labels for which reward zone was active ('A', 'B', 'C')\n    32→* `trial dict` is a dictionary with entries `trial_set0` and `trial_set1`, which are each boolean arrays indicating which trials were included in which trial set.\n    33→\n    34→Within multi_anim_sess['GCAMP4']['sess'], there are attributes: \n    35→```\n    36→dict_keys(['vr_data', 'trial_start_inds', 'teleport_inds', 'scan_info', 'n_planes', 'timeseries', 'trial_matrices', 'iscell', 'plane_per_cell', 's2p_ops', 's2p_stats', 'basedir_2P', 'basedir_VR', 'mouse', 'date', 'scene', 'session', 'scan_number', 'scanner', 'VR_only', 'scan_file', 'scanheader_file', 'vr_filename', 's2p_path', 'prompt_for_keys', 'verbose', 'basedir'])\n    37→```\n    38→\n    39→* `timeseries` is a dict containing the variables: dict_keys(['F', 'Fneu', 'licks', 'rewards', 'speed', 'events', 'dff'])\n    40→    *  each 'timeseries' entry is a cells x samples array, where each sample is a single imaging frame sampled at ~15.5 Hz (each frame is ~64.5 ms)\n    41→    * 'F' is the raw fluorescence of each cell ROI\n    42→    * 'Fneu' is the fluorescence in a small \"donut\" around the ROI that is subtracted in preprocessing before you get \"dff\"\n    43→    * 'dff' is delta F/F\n    44→    * 'events' is the deconvolved spike-like activity from 'dff' (but it's not really a spike rate, it just gets rid of the asymmetric smoothing of the calcium kernel)\n    45→    * 'licks' are a cumulative lick count in each frame, so in my behavior code anything >1 gets set to 1 to be able to convert it accurately to a rate when I want to convert it to Hz per position bin or time bin\n    46→    * 'rewards' is just a binary at the sample where reward was delivered\n    47→    * 'speed' is an actual smoothed running speed in cm/s for each sample.\n    48→* `trial_matrices` are the spatially binned versions of timeseries, in bins of 10 cm, from 0 to 450 cm (not including the teleport zone). Shape: trials x position bins x cells.\n    49→* `trial_start_inds` is a 1 x N trials array of the sample index for the start of each trial (i.e. the start of the track)\n    50→* `teleport_indices` is a 1 x N trials array of the sample index for the start of the teleport period (i.e. the end of the track)\n    51→* `vr_data` is a Pandas dataframe of VR behavior data converted from the sqlite files output by Unity and already synchronized with the imaging data\n    52→\n    53→The sess class, which aligns the VR data to the imaging data, is generated by [preprocessing.vr_align_to_2P](https://github.com/GiocomoLab/TwoPUtils/blob/00b6589e5afea4cfe2347557b0a2450d9673c435/TwoPUtils/preprocessing.py#L124) in the [TwoPUtils](https://github.com/GiocomoLab/TwoPUtils/tree/main) repo. Please review this function carefully to understand how the VR data are pre-processed and written into sess.vr_data.\n    54→\n    55→In multi_anim_sess['GCAMP4']['sess'].vr_data, there is one row per sample (imaging frame), and the relevant columns are:\n    56→* `time`: the timestamp of the sample, in the imaging frame rate (VR data have already been synchronized with imaging)\n    57→* `pos`: Animal's position in cm on a virtual linear track\n    58→* `trialnum`: trial number, where each trial is a lap of the track\n    59→* `dz`: delta in position from one sample to the next\n    60→* `speed`: the speed of the mouse in cm/s\n    61→* `lick`: lick detection by capacitive sensor; preprocessed cumulatively per imaging frame\n    62→* `reward`: a binary of when reward was delivered\n    63→* `rzone`: a binary flag of when the animal entered the reward zone; preprocessed cumulatively per imaging frame\n    64→* `morph`: the identity of the virtual environment (see above)\n    65→* `autoreward`: whether trial was automatically rewarded if the mouse failed to lick\n    66→* `tstart`: start of a trial, i.e. entry to the linear track\n    67→* `teleport`: end of a trial, i.e. entry to the linear track\n    68→* `scanning`: whether scanning occurred to collect imaging data (1 if scanning)\n    69→\n    70→All other vr_data columns are unused or can safely be ignored.\n    71→\n    72→In samples prior to when Unity started sending TTLs to the imaging computer to synchronize acquisition, `morph`, `trialnum`, and `scanning` have values set to -1, and `pos` is set to -500. \n    73→\n\n[metadata] {\"type\": \"text\", \"file\": {\"filePath\": \"/app/code/docs/multi_anim_sess_README.md\", \"content\": \"# Extended documentation for data stored in multi_anim_sess\\n\\nAs an example, let's take: multi_anim_sess file 2-3-4-6-7-10-11-12-13-14-15-17-18-19_expday3_speed2_perms100_maximin_events.pickle.\\n\\nThis filename means:\\n\\n* it includes mice m2, m3, m4, m6, m7, m10, m11, m12, m12, m13, m14, m15, m17, m18, m19. 2,3,4,7,11,12,13,14,15,17,18,19 learned the reward zone switch task.\\n* it contains data from experiment day 3 (the 1st switch day)\\n* place cells were identified taking neural activity at running speeds >2 cm/s\\n* 100 permutations were used for place cell significance calculations\\n* the dF/F baseline was calculated using a `maximin` filter\\n* deconvolved calcium `events` were used to define place cells\\n\\nEach pickle file is a collection of dictionaries, with a key for each animal, plus a key for the `params` used to define place cells. the VR data.\n   180→    Basically downsamples the VR data to the 2P framerate.\n   181→\n   182→    !! scan_info['frame_rate']  # frame rate\n   187→    lr = fr * scan_info['config']['lines'] / \\\n   188→        scan_info['fov_repeats']  # line rate\n   189→\n   190→    ttl_times = frames / fr + lines / lr\n   191→\n   192→    numVRFrames = frames.shape[0]\n   193→    # print('numVRFrames', numVRFrames)\n   194→\n   195→    # create empty pandas dataframe to store calcium aligned data\n   196→    ca_df = pd.DataFrame(columns=vr_dataframe.columns,\n   197→                         index=np.arange(int(scan_info['max_idx']/n_planes)))\n   198→    # ca_time = np.arange(0, 1 / fr * scan_info['max_idx'], 1 / fr)  # time on this even grid\n   199→    ca_time = np.arange(0, 1/fr * scan_info['max_idx'], n_planes/fr)\n   200→    ca_time[ca_time > ttl_times[-1]] = ttl_times[-1]\n   201→    print(f\"{ttl_times.shape} ttl times,{ca_time.shape} ca2+ frame times\")\n   202→    print(f\"last time: VR {ttl_times[-1]}, ca2+ {ca_time[-1]}\")\n   203→    # occasionally a 1 frame correction due to\n   204→    if (ca_time.shape[0] - ca_df.shape[0]) == 1:\n   205→        # scan stopping mid frame\n   206→        warnings.warn('one frame correction')\n   207→        ca_time = ca_time[:-1]\n   208→\n   209→    ca_df.loc[:, 'time'] = ca_time\n   210→    # mask for when ttls have started on imaging clock\n   211→    mask = ca_time >= ttl_times[0]\n   212→    # (i.e. imaging started and stabilized, ~10s)\n   213→\n   214→    # take VR frames for which there are valid TTLs\n   215→    vr_dataframe = vr_dataframe.iloc[-numVRFrames:]\n   216→\n   217→    # find columns that exist in sqlite file from iterable\n   218→    def column_filter(columns): return [\n   219→        col for col in vr_dataframe.columns if col in columns]\n   220→    # linear interpolation of position and catmull rom spline \"time\" parameter\n   221→    lin_interp_cols = column_filter(('pos', 'posx', 'posy', 't'))\n   222→\n   223→    f_mean = sp.interpolate.interp1d(\n   224→        ttl_times, vr_dataframe[lin_interp_cols]._values, axis=0, kind='slinear')\n   225→    ca_df.loc[mask, lin_interp_cols] = f_mean(ca_time[mask])\n   226→    ca_df.loc[~mask, 'pos'] = -500.\n   227→\n   228→    # nearest frame interpolation\n   229→    near_interp_cols = column_filter(('morph', 'towerJitter', 'wallJitter',\n   230→                                      'bckgndJitter', 'trialnum', 'cmd', 'scanning', 'dreamland', 'LR'))\n   231→\n   232→    f_nearest = sp.interpolate.interp1d(\n   233→        ttl_times, vr_dataframe[near_interp_cols]._values, axis=0, kind='nearest')\n   234→    ca_df.loc[mask, near_interp_cols] = f_nearest(ca_time[mask])\n   235→    ca_df.fillna(method='ffill', inplace=True)\n   236→    ca_df.loc[~mask, near_interp_cols] = -1.\n   237→\n   238→    # integrate, interpolate and then take difference, to make sure data is not lost\n   239→    cumsum_interp_cols = column_filter(\n   240→        ('dz', 'lick', 'reward', 'tstart', 'teleport', 'rzone'))\n   241→    f_cumsum = sp.interpolate.interp1d(ttl_times, np.cumsum(vr_dataframe[cumsum_interp_cols]._values, axis=0), axis=0,\n   242→                                       kind='slinear')\n   243→    ca_cumsum = np.round(np.insert(f_cumsum(ca_time[mask]), 0, [\n   244→                         0]*len(cumsum_interp_cols), axis=0))\n   245→    if ca_cumsum[-1, -2] < ca_cumsum[-1, -3]:\n   246→        ca_cumsum[-1, -2] += 1\n   247→\n   248→    ca_df.loc[mask, cumsum_interp_cols] = np.diff(ca_cumsum, axis=0)\n   249→    ca_df.loc[~mask, cumsum_interp_cols] = 0.\n   250→\n   251→    # fill na here\n   252→    ca_df.loc[np.isnan(ca_df['teleport']._values), 'teleport'] = 0\n   253→    ca_df.loc[np.isnan(ca_df['tstart']._values), 'tstart'] = 0\n   254→    # if first tstart gets clipped\n   255→    if ca_df['teleport'].sum(axis=0) != ca_df['tstart'].sum(axis=0):\n   256→        warnings.warn(\"Number of teleports and trial starts don't match\")\n   257→ Neuropil subtraction (coef=0.7) then add back neuropil mean per trial\\n    2. Maximin baseline within each trial (20s window = ~310 samples at 15.5 Hz)\\n    3. dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\\n    4. Smooth with 2-sample Gaussian\\n\\n    Returns dff array of same shape as F (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    n_neurons, n_samples = F.shape\\n\\n    # Neuropil subtraction\\n    F_corr = F - NEUROPIL_COEF * Fneu\\n\\n    dff = np.full_like(F_corr, np.nan)\\n\\n    window_size = int(20 * frame_rate)  # 20 seconds in samples (~310)\\n\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n\\n        # Add back neuropil mean per trial\\n        F_trial = F_corr[:, start:end] + NEUROPIL_COEF * np.nanmean(Fneu[:, start:end], axis=1, keepdims=True)\\n\\n        # Smooth for baseline calculation\\n        F_smooth = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(F_trial, 15, axis=1)\\n\\n        # Maximin baseline\\n        baseline = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(F_smooth, window_size, axis=1)\\n        baseline = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(baseline, window_size, axis=1)\\n\\n        # dF/F\\n        dff[:, start:end] = (F_trial - baseline) / np.abs(baseline + 1e-10)\\n\\n    # Smooth dF/F with 2-sample Gaussian kernel per trial\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n NaNs.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\\n    a_clean = np.copy(a)\\n    a_clean[nan_inds] = 0\\n\\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\\n\\n    a_smooth = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_clean, sig, axis=axis)\\n    one_smooth = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis)\\n\\n    return a_smooth / one_smooth\\n\\n\\ndef deconvolve_oasis(dff_trial, tau=TAU, frame_rate=FRAME_RATE):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Deconvolve dF/F using OASIS algorithm from suite2p.\\n    Falls back to simple threshold if suite2p not available.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n best_dist:\\n            best_dist = dist\\n            best_zone = zone\\n\\n    return best_zone\\n\\n\\ndef determine_trial_rewarded(position, rz_signal, reward_timestamps, timestamps,\\n                              trial_start, trial_end):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Determine if a trial was rewarded.\\n    A trial is rewarded if there's a reward delivery AND the mouse was in the reward zone.\\n Mask fluorescence to within-trial data only\\n    2. Neuropil subtraction (coef=0.7)\\n    3. Per trial: add back neuropil mean so baseline is close to true F\\n    4. Maximin baseline within each trial (300 sample window, matching reference)\\n    5. dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\\n    6. Smooth with 2-sample Gaussian\\n\\n    Returns dff array of shape (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    n_neurons, n_samples = F.shape\\n\\n    # Initialize masked arrays (NaN outside trials, matching reference code)\\n    f_ = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\\n    f_neu_ = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\\n\\n    # Mask to within-trial data only\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n        f_[:, start:end] = F[:, start:end]\\n        f_neu_[:, start:end] = Fneu[:, start:end]\\n\\n    # Neuropil subtraction on masked data\\n    nanmask = ~np.isnan(f_[0, :])\\n    f_[:, nanmask] = f_[:, nanmask] - NEUROPIL_COEF * f_neu_[:, nanmask]\\n\\n    # Baseline and dF/F per trial\\n    dff = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\\n    window_size = 300  # ~20s at 15.5 Hz, matching reference code int(300)\\n\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n\\n        # Add back neuropil mean per trial\\n        # (so dff values are close to true dF/F and we don't divide by small numbers)\\n        f_[:, start:end] = f_[:, start:end] + NEUROPIL_COEF * np.nanmean(\\n            f_neu_[:, start:end], axis=1, keepdims=True)\\n\\n        # Smooth for baseline calculation (sigma=15 samples along time)\\n        f_smooth = nansmooth(f_[:, start:end], 15, axis=1)\\n\\n        # Maximin baseline\\n        baseline = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(f_smooth, window_size, axis=-1)\\n        baseline = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(baseline, window_size, axis=-1)\\n\\n        # dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\\n        dff[:, start:end] = (f_[:, start:end] - baseline) / np.abs(baseline)\\n\\n    # Smooth dF/F with 2-sample Gaussian kernel per trial\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n        dff[:, start:end] = nansmooth(dff[:, start:end], 2, axis=1)\\n\\n    return dff\", \"originalFile\": \"#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nConvert NWB data from Sosa, Plitt, & Giocomo (2025) into the decoder format.\\n\\nProcessing pipeline (matching reference paper and code):\\n1. Format into decoder structure\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport os\\nimport sys\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pickle\\nimport warnings\\nfrom glob import glob\\nfrom scipy import ndimage, interpolate\\n\\nwarnings.filterwarnings('ignore')\\n\\n# ---- Configuration ----\\nDATA_DIR = 'data'\\nFRAME_RATE = 15.5078125  # Hz, from NWB files\\nTIME_BIN_MS = 1000.0 / FRAME_RATE  # ~64.5 ms\\nTRACK_LENGTH = 450  # cm\\nSPEED_THR = 2.0  # cm/s for place cell analyses (not applied to decoder data)\\nNEUROPIL_COEF = 0.7\\nTAU = 0.7  # calcium indicator decay constant for OASIS\\nINTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THR = 0.5\\n\\n# Reward zone definitions from behavior.py\\nREWARD_ZONES = {\\n    'A': [80, 130],\\n    'B': [200, 250],\\n    'C': [320, 370],\\n}\\n\\n\\ndef load_nwb(filepath):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load NWB file and extract all needed data.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    from pynwb import NWBHDF5IO\\n\\n    io = NWBHDF5IO(filepath, 'r')\\n    nwb = io.read()\\n\\n    bts = nwb.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries']\\n    ophys = nwb.processing['ophys']\\n    seg = ophys['ImageSegmentation']['PlaneSegmentation']\\n\\n    data = {\\n        'position': np.array(bts.time_series['position'].data[:]),\\n        'speed': np.array(bts.time_series['speed'].data[:]),\\n        'lick': np.array(bts.time_series['lick'].data[:]),\\n        'reward_zone': np.array(bts.time_series['reward_zone'].data[:]),\\n        'teleport': np.array(bts.time_series['teleport'].data[:]),\\n        'trial_start': np.array(bts.time_series['trial_start'].data[:]),\\n        'trial_number': np.array(bts.time_series['trial number'].data[:]),\\n        'environment': np.array(bts.time_series['environment'].data[:]),\\n        'scanning': np.array(bts.time_series['scanning'].data[:]),\\n        'autoreward': np.array(bts.time_series['autoreward'].data[:]),\\n        'timestamps': np.array(bts.time_series['position'].timestamps[:]),\\n        # Reward is event-based with timestamps\\n        'reward_timestamps': np.array(bts.time_series['Reward'].timestamps[:]),\\n        'reward_data': np.array(bts.time_series['Reward'].data[:]),\\n        # Neural data - shape: (n_timepoints, n_neurons)\\n        'fluorescence': np.array(ophys['Fluorescence']['plane0'].data[:]),\\n        'neuropil': np.array(ophys['Neuropil']['plane0'].data[:]),\\n        'deconvolved': np.array(ophys['Deconvolved']['plane0'].data[:]),\\n        # Cell info\\n        'iscell': np.array(seg['iscell'].data[:]),\\n        'plane_idx': np.array(seg['planeIdx'].data[:]),\\n        # Metadata\\n        'session_id': nwb.session_id,\\n        'subject_id': nwb.subject.subject_id if nwb.subject else None,\\n    }\\n\\n    io.close()\\n    return data\\n\\n\\ndef compute_dff_per_trial(F, Fneu, trial_starts, trial_ends, frame_rate=FRAME_RATE):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Compute dF/F following the paper's method:\\n    1. Neuropil subtraction (coef=0.7) then add back neuropil mean per trial\\n    2. Maximin baseline within each trial (20s window = ~310 samples at 15.5 Hz)\\n    3. dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\\n    4. Smooth with 2-sample Gaussian\\n\\n    Returns dff array of same shape as F (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    n_neurons, n_samples = F.shape\\n\\n    # Neuropil subtraction\\n    F_corr = F - NEUROPIL_COEF * Fneu\\n\\n    dff = np.full_like(F_corr, np.nan)\\n\\n    window_size = int(20 * frame_rate)  # 20 seconds in samples (~310)\\n\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n\\n        # Add back neuropil mean per trial\\n        F_trial = F_corr[:, start:end] + NEUROPIL_COEF * np.nanmean(Fneu[:, start:end], axis=1, keepdims=True)\\n\\n        # Smooth for baseline calculation\\n        F_smooth = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(F_trial, 15, axis=1)\\n\\n        # Maximin baseline\\n        baseline = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(F_smooth, window_size, axis=1)\\n        baseline = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(baseline, window_size, axis=1)\\n\\n        # dF/F\\n        dff[:, start:end] = (F_trial - baseline) / np.abs(baseline + 1e-10)\\n\\n    # Smooth dF/F with 2-sample Gaussian kernel per trial\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n        dff[:, start:end] = nansmooth(dff[:, start:end], 2, axis=1)\\n\\n    return dff\\n\\n\\ndef nansmooth(a, sig, axis=-1):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Gaussian smoothing that handles NaNs.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\\n    a_clean = np.copy(a)\\n    a_clean[nan_inds] = 0\\n\\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\\n\\n    a_smooth = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_clean, sig, axis=axis)\\n    one_smooth = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis)\\n\\n    return a_smooth / one_smooth\\n\\n\\ndef deconvolve_oasis(dff_trial, tau=TAU, frame_rate=FRAME_RATE):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Deconvolve dF/F using OASIS algorithm from suite2p.\\n    Falls back to simple threshold if suite2p not available.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    try:\\n        from suite2p.extraction.dcnv import oasis\\n        # oasis(F, batch_size, tau, fs)\\n        events = oasis(dff_trial, 2000, tau, frame_rate)\\n        return events\\n    except ImportError:\\n        # Fallback: simple non-negative deconvolution\\n        events = np.copy(dff_trial)\\n        events[events < 0] = 0\\n        return events\\n\\n\\ndef filter_interneurons(dff, speed, iscell_mask):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Filter putative interneurons by correlation with running speed > 0.5.\\n    Returns updated mask.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    mask = np.copy(iscell_mask)\\n    valid = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :]) & ~np.isnan(speed)\\n\\n    if valid.sum() < 10:\\n        return mask\\n\\n    cell_indices = np.where(mask)[0]\\n    for idx in cell_indices:\\n        cell_data = dff[idx, valid]\\n        speed_data = speed[valid]\\n        if np.std(cell_data) > 0 and np.std(speed_data) > 0:\\n            corr = np.corrcoef(cell_data, speed_data)[0, 1]\\n            if corr > INTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THR:\\n                mask[idx] = False\\n\\n    return mask\\n\\n\\ndef get_trial_boundaries(trial_start_signal, teleport_signal, trial_numbers, scanning):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Get trial start and end indices from the behavioral signals.\\n    Returns list of (start_idx, end_idx) for each valid trial.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Find trial start indices\\n    start_indices = np.where(trial_start_signal > 0)[0]\\n    # Find teleport (trial end) indices\\n    end_indices = np.where(teleport_signal > 0)[0]\\n\\n    if len(start_indices) == 0 or len(end_indices) == 0:\\n        return [], []\\n\\n    # Match starts and ends\\n    trial_starts = []\\n    trial_ends = []\\n\\n    for s in start_indices:\\n        # Find the next teleport after this start\\n        next_ends = end_indices[end_indices > s]\\n        if len(next_ends) > 0:\\n            e = next_ends[0]\\n            # Only include if scanning was on (scanning == 1)\\n            if np.any(scanning[s:e] == 1):\\n                trial_starts.append(s)\\n                trial_ends.append(e)\\n\\n    return trial_starts, trial_ends\\n\\n\\ndef determine_reward_zone_label(position, rz_signal, trial_start, trial_end):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Determine which reward zone (A, B, or C) is active for a trial\\n    based on positions where reward_zone signal > 0.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    pos_trial = position[trial_start:trial_end]\\n    rz_trial = rz_signal[trial_start:trial_end]\\n\\n    rz_positions = pos_trial[rz_trial > 0]\\n\\n    if len(rz_positions) == 0:\\n        \n\n========================================================================================================================\n\nSTEP 53\nPATTERN Mask fluorescence to within-trial data only\\n    2. Neuropil subtraction (coef=0.7)\\n    3. Per trial: add back neuropil mean so baseline is close to true F\\n    4. Maximin baseline within each trial (300 sample window, matching reference)\\n    5. dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\\n    6. Smooth with 2-sample Gaussian\\n\\n    Returns dff array of shape (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    n_neurons, n_samples = F.shape\\n\\n    # Initialize masked arrays (NaN outside trials, matching reference code)\\n    f_ = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\\n    f_neu_ = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\\n\\n    # Mask to within-trial data only\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n        f_[:, start:end] = F[:, start:end]\\n        f_neu_[:, start:end] = Fneu[:, start:end]\\n\\n    # Metadata\\n        'session_id': nwb.session_id,\\n        'subject_id': nwb.subject.subject_id if nwb.subject else None,\\n        'n_planes': n_planes,\\n    }\\n\\n    io.close()\\n    return data\\n\\n\\ndef compute_dff_per_trial(F, Fneu, trial_starts, trial_ends, frame_rate=FRAME_RATE):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Compute dF/F following the paper's method (matching preprocessing.py):\\n    1. Mask fluorescence to within-trial data only\\n    2. Neuropil subtraction (coef=0.7)\\n    3. Per trial: add back neuropil mean so baseline is close to true F\\n    4. Maximin baseline within each trial (300 sample window, matching reference)\\n    5. dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\\n    6. Smooth with 2-sample Gaussian\\n\\n    Returns dff array of shape (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    n_neurons, n_samples = F.shape\\n\\n    # Initialize masked arrays (NaN outside trials, matching reference code)\\n    f_ = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\\n    f_neu_ = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\\n\\n    # Mask to within-trial data only\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n        f_[:, start:end] = F[:, start:end]\\n        f_neu_[:, start:end] = Fneu[:, start:end]\\n\\n    # Neuropil subtraction on masked data\\n    nanmask = ~np.isnan(f_[0, :])\\n    f_[:, nanmask] = f_[:, nanmask] - NEUROPIL_COEF * f_neu_[:, nanmask]\\n\\n    # Baseline and dF/F per trial\\n    dff = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\\n    window_size = 300  # ~20s at 15.5 Hz, matching reference code int(300)\\n\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n\\n        # Add back neuropil mean per trial\\n        # (so dff values are close to true dF/F and we don't divide by small numbers)\\n        f_[:, start:end] = f_[:, start:end] + NEUROPIL_COEF * np.nanmean(\\n            f_neu_[:, start:end], axis=1, keepdims=True)\\n\\n        # Smooth for baseline calculation (sigma=15 samples along time)\\n        f_smooth = nansmooth(f_[:, start:end], 15, axis=1)\\n\\n        # Maximin baseline\\n        baseline = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(f_smooth, window_size, axis=-1)\\n        baseline = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(baseline, window_size, axis=-1)\\n\\n        # dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\\n        dff[:, start:end] = (f_[:, start:end] - baseline) / np.abs(baseline)\\n\\n    # Smooth dF/F with 2-sample Gaussian kernel per trial\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n        dff[:, start:end] = nansmooth(dff[:, start:end], 2, axis=1)\\n\\n    return dff\\n\\n\\ndef nansmooth(a, sig, axis=-1):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Gaussian smoothing that handles NaNs.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\\n    a_clean = np.copy(a)\\n    a_clean[nan_inds] = 0\\n\\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\\n\\n    a_smooth = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_clean, sig, axis=axis)\\n    one_smooth = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis)\\n\\n    return a_smooth / one_smooth\\n\\n\\ndef deconvolve_oasis(dff_trial, tau=TAU, frame_rate=FRAME_RATE):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Deconvolve dF/F using OASIS algorithm from suite2p.\\n    Falls back to simple threshold if suite2p not available.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    try:\\n        from suite2p.extraction.dcnv import oasis\\n        # oasis(F, batch_size, tau, fs)\\n        events = oasis(dff_trial, 2000, tau, frame_rate)\\n        return events\\n    except ImportError:\\n        # Fallback: simple non-negative deconvolution\\n        events = np.copy(dff_trial)\\n        events[events < 0] = 0\\n        return events\\n\\n\\ndef filter_interneurons(dff, speed, iscell_mask):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Filter putative interneurons by correlation with running speed > 0.5.\\n    Returns updated mask.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    mask = np.copy(iscell_mask)\\n    valid = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :]) & ~np.isnan(speed)\\n\\n    if valid.sum() < 10:\\n        return mask\\n\\n    cell_indices = np.where(mask)[0]\\n    for idx in cell_indices:\\n        cell_data = dff[idx, valid]\\n        speed_data = speed[valid]\\n        if np.std(cell_data) > 0 and np.std(speed_data) > 0:\\n            corr = np.corrcoef(cell_data, speed_data)[0, 1]\\n            if corr > INTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THR:\\n                mask[idx] = False\\n\\n    return mask\\n\\n\\ndef get_trial_boundaries(trial_start_signal, teleport_signal, trial_numbers, scanning):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Get trial start and end indices from the behavioral signals.\\n    Returns list of (start_idx, end_idx) for each valid trial.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Find trial start indices\\n    start_indices = np.where(trial_start_signal > 0)[0]\\n    # Find teleport (trial end) indices\\n    end_indices = np.where(teleport_signal > 0)[0]\\n\\n    if len(start_indices) == 0 or len(end_indices) == 0:\\n        return [], []\\n\\n    # Match starts Hz, from NWB files\\nTIME_BIN_MS = 1000.0 / FRAME_RATE  # ~64.5 ms\\nTRACK_LENGTH = 450  # cm\\nSPEED_THR = 2.0  # cm/s for place cell analyses (not applied to decoder data)\\nNEUROPIL_COEF = 0.7\\nTAU = 0.7  # calcium indicator decay constant for OASIS\\nINTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THR = 0.5\\n\\n# Reward zone definitions from behavior.py\\nREWARD_ZONES = {\\n    'A': [80, 130],\\n    'B': [200, 250],\\n    'C': [320, 370],\\n}\\n\\n\\ndef load_nwb(filepath):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load NWB file and extract all needed data.\\n    Handles both single-plane and multi-plane recordings.\\n    Multi-plane: pools ROIs across planes (matching paper methods).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    from pynwb import NWBHDF5IO\\n\\n    io = NWBHDF5IO(filepath, 'r')\\n    nwb = io.read()\\n\\n    bts = nwb.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries']\\n    ophys = nwb.processing['ophys']\\n    seg = ophys['ImageSegmentation']['PlaneSegmentation']\\n\\n    # Determine number of planes\\n    plane_keys = sorted(ophys['Fluorescence'].roi_response_series.keys())\\n    n_planes = len(plane_keys)\\n\\n    # Load and concatenate neural data across planes\\n    if n_planes == 1:\\n        fluorescence = np.array(ophys['Fluorescence']['plane0'].data[:])\\n        neuropil_data = np.array(ophys['Neuropil']['plane0'].data[:])\\n        deconvolved = np.array(ophys['Deconvolved']['plane0'].data[:])\\n    else:\\n        # Multi-plane: concatenate neurons from all planes\\n        # Paper: \\\"ROIs were identified separately per plane, but planes were pooled for all analyses\\\"\\n        fluor_planes = [np.array(ophys['Fluorescence'][k].data[:]) for k in plane_keys]\\n        neuro_planes = [np.array(ophys['Neuropil'][k].data[:]) for k in plane_keys]\\n        deconv_planes = [np.array(ophys['Deconvolved'][k].data[:]) for k in plane_keys]\\n        fluorescence = np.concatenate(fluor_planes, axis=1)\\n        neuropil_data = np.concatenate(neuro_planes, axis=1)\\n        deconvolved = np.concatenate(deconv_planes, axis=1)\\n\\n    data = {\\n        'position': np.array(bts.time_series['position'].data[:]),\\n        'speed': np.array(bts.time_series['speed'].data[:]),\\n        'lick': np.array(bts.time_series['lick'].data[:]),\\n        'reward_zone': np.array(bts.time_series['reward_zone'].data[:]),\\n        'teleport': np.array(bts.time_series['teleport'].data[:]),\\n        'trial_start': np.array(bts.time_series['trial_start'].data[:]),\\n        'trial_number': np.array(bts.time_series['trial number'].data[:]),\\n        'environment': np.array(bts.time_series['environment'].data[:]),\\n        'scanning': np.array(bts.time_series['scanning'].data[:]),\\n        'autoreward': np.array(bts.time_series['autoreward'].data[:]),\\n        'timestamps': np.array(bts.time_series['position'].timestamps[:]),\\n        'reward_timestamps': np.array(bts.time_series['Reward'].timestamps[:]),\\n        'reward_data': np.array(bts.time_series['Reward'].data[:]),\\n        # Neural data - shape: (n_timepoints, n_neurons)\\n        'fluorescence': fluorescence,\\n        'neuropil': neuropil_data,\\n        'deconvolved': deconvolved,\\n        # Cell info\\n        'iscell': np.array(seg['iscell'].data[:]),\\n        'plane_idx': np.array(seg['planeIdx'].data[:]),\\n        # Metadata\\n        'session_id': nwb.session_id,\\n        'subject_id': nwb.subject.subject_id if nwb.subject else None,\\n        'n_planes': n_planes,\\n    }\\n\\n    io.close()\\n    return data\\n\\n\\ndef compute_dff_per_trial(F, Fneu, trial_starts, trial_ends, frame_rate=FRAME_RATE):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Compute dF/F following the paper's method (matching preprocessing.py):\\n    1. Mask fluorescence to within-trial data only\\n    2. Neuropil subtraction (coef=0.7)\\n    3. Per trial: add back neuropil mean so baseline is close to true F\\n    4. Maximin baseline within each trial (300 sample window, matching reference)\\n    5. dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\\n    6. Smooth with 2-sample Gaussian\\n\\n    Returns dff array of shape (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    n_neurons, n_samples = F.shape\\n\\n    # Initialize masked arrays (NaN outside trials, matching reference code)\\n    f_ = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\\n    f_neu_ = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\\n\\n    # Mask to within-trial data only\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n        f_[:, start:end] = F[:, start:end]\\n        f_neu_[:, start:end] = Fneu[:, start:end]\\n\\n    # Neuropil subtraction on masked data\\n    nanmask = ~np.isnan(f_[0, :])\\n    f_[:, nanmask] = f_[:, nanmask] - NEUROPIL_COEF * f_neu_[:, nanmask]\\n\\n    # Baseline and dF/F per trial\\n    dff = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\\n    window_size = 300  # ~20s at 15.5 Hz, matching reference code int(300)\\n\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n\\n        # Add back neuropil mean per trial\\n        # (so dff values are close to true dF/F and we don't divide by small numbers)\\n        f_[:, start:end] = f_[:, start:end] + NEUROPIL_COEF * np.nanmean(\\n            f_neu_[:, start:end], axis=1, keepdims=True)\\n\\n        # Smooth for baseline calculation (sigma=15 samples along time)\\n        f_smooth = nansmooth(f_[:, start:end], 15, axis=1)\\n\\n        # Maximin baseline\\n        baseline = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(f_smooth, window_size, axis=-1)\\n        baseline = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(baseline, window_size, axis=-1)\\n\\n        # dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\\n        dff[:, start:end] = (f_[:, start:end] - baseline) / np.abs(baseline)\\n\\n    # Smooth dF/F with 2-sample Gaussian kernel per trial\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n        dff[:, start:end] = nansmooth(dff[:, start:end], 2, axis=1)\\n\\n    return dff\\n\\n\\ndef nansmooth(a, sig, axis=-1):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Gaussian smoothing that handles NaNs.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\\n    a_clean = np.copy(a)\\n    a_clean[nan_inds] = 0\\n\\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\\n\\n    a_smooth = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_clean, sig, axis=axis)\\n    one_smooth = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis)\\n\\n    return a_smooth / one_smooth\\n\\n\\ndef deconvolve_oasis(dff_trial, tau=TAU, frame_rate=FRAME_RATE):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Deconvolve dF/F using OASIS algorithm from suite2p.\\n    Falls back to simple threshold if suite2p not available.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    try:\\n        from suite2p.extraction.dcnv import oasis\\n        # oasis(F, batch_size, tau, fs)\\n        events = oasis(dff_trial, 2000, tau, frame_rate)\\n        return events\\n    except ImportError:\\n        # Fallback: simple non-negative deconvolution\\n        events = np.copy(dff_trial)\\n        events[events < 0] = 0\\n        return events\\n\\n\\ndef filter_interneurons(dff, speed, iscell_mask):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Filter putative interneurons by correlation with running speed > 0.5.\\n    Returns updated mask.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    mask = np.copy(iscell_mask)\\n    valid = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :]) & ~np.isnan(speed)\\n\\n    if valid.sum() < 10:\\n        return mask\\n\\n    cell_indices = np.where(mask)[0]\\n    for idx in cell_indices:\\n        cell_data = dff[idx, valid]\\n        speed_data = speed[valid]\\n        if np.std(cell_data) > 0 and np.std(speed_data) > 0:\\n            corr = np.corrcoef(cell_data, speed_data)[0, 1]\\n            if corr > INTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THR:\\n                mask[idx] = False\\n\\n    return mask\\n\\n\\ndef get_trial_boundaries(trial_start_signal, teleport_signal, trial_numbers, scanning):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Get trial start and end indices from the behavioral signals.\\n    Returns list of (start_idx, end_idx) for each valid trial.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Find trial start indices\\n    start_indices = np.where(trial_start_signal > 0)[0]\\n    # Find teleport (trial end) indices\\n    end_indices = np.where(teleport_signal > 0)[0]\\n\\n    if len(start_indices) == 0 or len(end_indices) == 0:\\n        return [], []\\n\\n    # Match starts and ends\\n    trial_starts = []\\n    trial_ends = []\\n\\n    for s in start_indices:\\n        # Find the next teleport after this start\\n        next_ends = end_indices[end_indices > s]\\n        if len(next_ends) > 0:\\n            e = next_ends[0]\\n            # Only include if scanning was on (scanning == 1)\\n            if np.any(scanning[s:e] == 1):\\n                trial_starts.append(s)\\n                trial_ends.append(e)\\n\\n    return trial_starts, trial_ends\\n\\n\\ndef determine_reward_zone_label(position, rz_signal, trial_start, trial_end):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Determine which reward zone (A, B, or C) is active for a trial\\n    based on positions where reward_zone signal > 0.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    pos_trial = position[trial_start:trial_end]\\n    rz_trial = rz_signal[trial_start:trial_end]\\n\\n    rz_positions = pos_trial[rz_trial > 0]\\n\\n    if len(rz_positions) == 0:\\n        return None  # Can't determine from this trial alone\\n\\n    mean_rz_pos = np.mean(rz_positions)\\n\\n    # Match to closest zone\\n    best_zone = None\\n    best_dist = float('inf')\\n    for zone, (start, end) in REWARD_ZONES.items():\\n        zone_center = (start + end) / 2\\n        dist = abs(mean_rz_pos - zone_center)\\n        if dist < best_dist:\\n            best_dist = dist\\n            best_zone = zone\\n\\n    return best_zone\\n\\n\\ndef determine_trial_rewarded(position, rz_signal, reward_timestamps, timestamps,\\n                              trial_start, trial_end):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Determine if a trial was rewarded.\\n    A trial is rewarded if there's a reward delivery AND the mouse was in the reward zone.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    t_start = timestamps[trial_start]\\n    t_end = timestamps[min(trial_end, len(timestamps) - 1)]\\n\\n    # Check if any reward was delivered during this trial\\n    reward_in_trial = np.any(\\n        (reward_timestamps >= t_start) & (reward_timestamps <= t_end)\\n    )\\n\\n    \n\n========================================================================================================================\n\nSTEP 74\nPATTERN calcium indicator decay constant for OASIS\\nINTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THR = 0.5\\n\\n# Reward zone definitions from behavior.py\\nREWARD_ZONES = {\\n    'A': [80, 130],\\n    'B': [200, 250],\\n    'C': [320, 370],\\n}\\n\\n\\ndef load_nwb(filepath):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load NWB file and extract all needed data.\\n    Handles both single-plane and multi-plane recordings.\\n    Multi-plane: pools ROIs across planes (matching paper methods).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    from pynwb import NWBHDF5IO\\n\\n    io = NWBHDF5IO(filepath, 'r')\\n    nwb = io.read()\\n\\n    bts = nwb.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries']\\n    ophys = nwb.processing['ophys']\\n    seg = ophys['ImageSegmentation']['PlaneSegmentation']\\n\\n    # Determine number of planes\\n    plane_keys = sorted(ophys['Fluorescence'].roi_response_series.keys())\\n    n_planes = len(plane_keys)\\n\\n    # Load and concatenate neural data across planes\\n    if n_planes == 1:\\n        fluorescence = np.array(ophys['Fluorescence']['plane0'].data[:])\\n        neuropil_data = np.array(ophys['Neuropil']['plane0'].data[:])\\n        deconvolved = np.array(ophys['Deconvolved']['plane0'].data[:])\\n    else:\\n        # Multi-plane: concatenate neurons from all planes\\n        # Paper: \\\"ROIs were identified separately per plane, but planes were pooled for all analyses\\\"\\n        fluor_planes = [np.array(ophys['Fluorescence'][k].data[:]) for k in plane_keys]\\n        neuro_planes = [np.array(ophys['Neuropil'][k].data[:]) for k in plane_keys]\\n        deconv_planes = [np.array(ophys['Deconvolved'][k].data[:]) for k in plane_keys]\\n        fluorescence = np.concatenate(fluor_planes, axis=1)\\n        neuropil_data = np.concatenate(neuro_planes, axis=1)\\n        deconvolved = np.concatenate(deconv_planes, axis=1)\\n\\n    data = {\\n        'position': np.array(bts.time_series['position'].data[:]),\\n        'speed': np.array(bts.time_series['speed'].data[:]),\\n        'lick': np.array(bts.time_series['lick'].data[:]),\\n        'reward_zone': np.array(bts.time_series['reward_zone'].data[:]),\\n        'teleport': np.array(bts.time_series['teleport'].data[:]),\\n        'trial_start': np.array(bts.time_series['trial_start'].data[:]),\\n        'trial_number': np.array(bts.time_series['trial number'].data[:]),\\n        'environment': np.array(bts.time_series['environment'].data[:]),\\n        'scanning': np.array(bts.time_series['scanning'].data[:]),\\n        'autoreward': np.array(bts.time_series['autoreward'].data[:]),\\n        'timestamps': np.array(bts.time_series['position'].timestamps[:]),\\n        'reward_timestamps': np.array(bts.time_series['Reward'].timestamps[:]),\\n        'reward_data': np.array(bts.time_series['Reward'].data[:]),\\n        # Neural data - shape: (n_timepoints, n_neurons)\\n        'fluorescence': fluorescence,\\n        'neuropil': neuropil_data,\\n        'deconvolved': deconvolved,\\n        # Cell info\\n        'iscell': np.array(seg['iscell'].data[:]),\\n        'plane_idx': np.array(seg['planeIdx'].data[:]),\\n        # Metadata\\n        'session_id': nwb.session_id,\\n        'subject_id': nwb.subject.subject_id if nwb.subject else None,\\n        'n_planes': n_planes,\\n    }\\n\\n    io.close()\\n    return data\\n\\n\\ndef compute_dff_per_trial(F, Fneu, trial_starts, trial_ends, frame_rate=FRAME_RATE):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Compute dF/F following the paper's method (matching preprocessing.py):\\n    1. Mask fluorescence to within-trial data only\\n    2. Neuropil subtraction (coef=0.7)\\n    3. Per trial: add back neuropil mean so baseline is close to true F\\n    4. Maximin baseline within each trial (300 sample window, matching reference)\\n    5. dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\\n    6. Smooth with 2-sample Gaussian\\n\\n    Returns dff array of shape (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    n_neurons, n_samples = F.shape\\n\\n    # Initialize masked arrays (NaN outside trials, matching reference code)\\n    f_ = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\\n    f_neu_ = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\\n\\n    # Mask to within-trial data only\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n        f_[:, start:end] = F[:, start:end]\\n        f_neu_[:, start:end] = Fneu[:, start:end]\\n\\n    # Neuropil subtraction on masked data\\n    nanmask = ~np.isnan(f_[0, :])\\n    f_[:, nanmask] = f_[:, nanmask] - NEUROPIL_COEF * f_neu_[:, nanmask]\\n\\n    # Baseline and dF/F per trial\\n    dff = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\\n    window_size = 300  # ~20s at 15.5 Hz, matching reference code int(300)\\n\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n\\n        # Add back neuropil mean per trial\\n        # (so dff values are close to true dF/F and we don't divide by small numbers)\\n        f_[:, start:end] = f_[:, start:end] + NEUROPIL_COEF * np.nanmean(\\n            f_neu_[:, start:end], axis=1, keepdims=True)\\n\\n        # Smooth for baseline calculation (sigma=15 samples along time)\\n        f_smooth = nansmooth(f_[:, start:end], 15, axis=1)\\n\\n        # Maximin baseline\\n        baseline = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(f_smooth, window_size, axis=-1)\\n        baseline = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(baseline, window_size, axis=-1)\\n\\n        # dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\\n        dff[:, start:end] = (f_[:, start:end] - baseline) / np.abs(baseline)\\n\\n    # Smooth dF/F with 2-sample Gaussian kernel per trial\\n    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n        if end <= start:\\n            continue\\n        dff[:, start:end] = nansmooth(dff[:, start:end], 2, axis=1)\\n\\n    return dff\\n\\n\\ndef nansmooth(a, sig, axis=-1):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Gaussian smoothing that handles NaNs.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\\n    a_clean = np.copy(a)\\n    a_clean[nan_inds] = 0\\n\\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\\n\\n    a_smooth = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_clean, sig, axis=axis)\\n    one_smooth = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis)\\n\\n    return a_smooth / one_smooth\\n\\n\\ndef deconvolve_oasis(dff_trial, tau=TAU, frame_rate=FRAME_RATE):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Deconvolve dF/F using OASIS algorithm from suite2p.\\n    Falls back to simple threshold if suite2p not available.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    try:\\n        from suite2p.extraction.dcnv import oasis\\n        # oasis(F, batch_size, tau, fs)\\n        events = oasis(dff_trial, 2000, tau, frame_rate)\\n        return events\\n    except ImportError:\\n        # Fallback: simple non-negative deconvolution\\n        events = np.copy(dff_trial)\\n        events[events < 0] = 0\\n        return events\\n\\n\\ndef filter_interneurons(dff, speed, iscell_mask):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Filter putative interneurons by correlation with running speed > 0.5.\\n    Returns updated mask.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    mask = np.copy(iscell_mask)\\n    valid = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :]) & ~np.isnan(speed)\\n\\n    if valid.sum() < 10:\\n        return mask\\n\\n    cell_indices = np.where(mask)[0]\\n    for idx in cell_indices:\\n        cell_data = dff[idx, valid]\\n        speed_data = speed[valid]\\n        if np.std(cell_data) > 0 and np.std(speed_data) > 0:\\n            corr = np.corrcoef(cell_data, speed_data)[0, 1]\\n            if corr > INTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THR:\\n                mask[idx] = False\\n\\n    return mask\\n\\n\\ndef get_trial_boundaries(trial_start_signal, teleport_signal, trial_numbers, scanning):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Get trial start and end indices from the behavioral signals.\\n    Returns list of (start_idx, end_idx) for each valid trial.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Find trial start indices\\n    start_indices = np.where(trial_start_signal > 0)[0]\\n    # Find teleport (trial end) indices\\n    end_indices = np.where(teleport_signal > 0)[0]\\n\\n    if len(start_indices) == 0 or len(end_indices) == 0:\\n        return [], []\\n\\n    # Match starts and ends\\n    trial_starts = []\\n    trial_ends = []\\n\\n    for s in start_indices:\\n        # Find the next teleport after this start\\n        next_ends = end_indices[end_indices > s]\\n        if len(next_ends) > 0:\\n            e = next_ends[0]\\n            # Only include if scanning was on (scanning == 1)\\n            if np.any(scanning[s:e] == 1):\\n                trial_starts.append(s)\\n                trial_ends.append(e)\\n\\n    return trial_starts, trial_ends\\n\\n\\ndef determine_reward_zone_label(position, rz_signal, trial_start, trial_end):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Determine which reward zone (A, B, or C) is active for a trial\\n    based on positions where reward_zone signal > 0.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    pos_trial = position[trial_start:trial_end]\\n    rz_trial = rz_signal[trial_start:trial_end]\\n\\n    rz_positions = pos_trial[rz_trial > 0]\\n\\n    if len(rz_positions) == 0:\\n        return None  # Can't determine from this trial alone\\n\\n    mean_rz_pos = np.mean(rz_positions)\\n\\n    # Match to closest zone\\n    best_zone = None\\n    best_dist = float('inf')\\n    for zone, (start, end) in REWARD_ZONES.items():\\n        zone_center = (start + end) / 2\\n        dist = abs(mean_rz_pos - zone_center)\\n        if dist < best_dist:\\n            best_dist = dist\\n            best_zone = zone\\n\\n    return best_zone\\n\\n\\ndef determine_trial_rewarded(position, rz_signal, reward_timestamps, timestamps,\\n                              trial_start, trial_end):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Determine if a trial was rewarded.\\n    A trial is rewarded if there's a reward delivery AND the mouse was in the reward zone.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    t_start = timestamps[trial_start]\\n    t_end = timestamps[min(trial_end, len(timestamps) - 1)]\\n\\n    # Check if any reward was delivered during this trial\\n    reward_in_trial = np.any(\\n        (reward_timestamps >= t_start) & (reward_timestamps <= t_end)\\n    )\\n\\n    # Check if mouse entered reward zone\\n    rz_trial = rz_signal[trial_start:trial_end]\\n    entered_rz = np.any(rz_trial > 0)\\n\\n    return int(reward_in_trial and entered_rz)\\n\\n\\ndef determine_reward_zone_for_all_trials(position, rz_signal, trial_starts, trial_ends):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Determine reward zone label for each trial.\\n    For trials where the mouse didn't enter the zone (omission),\\n    infer from neighborin\n\n========================================================================================================================\n\nSTEP 113\nPATTERN Data\\n\\n**Paper**: \\\"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\\\"\\nSosa, Plitt, & Giocomo (2025)\\n\\n**Data**: NWB files from 11 switch-condition mice (m3, m4, m7, m11-m15, m17-m19), 12-14 sessions each (152 total sessions, 12,216 trials).\\n\\n**Imaging**: 2-photon calcium imaging (GCaMP7f) of hippocampal CA1 neurons at 15.5078125 Hz (~64.5 ms time bins). Most mice have single-plane recordings; m17 and m18 have 2-plane recordings (pooled across planes per paper methods).\\n\\n## Processing Pipeline\\n\\n### 1. Cell Selection\\n- Used `iscell[:, 0] == 1` from suite2p curation to select valid ROIs.\\n\\n### 2. dF/F Computation (matching `preprocessing.py`)\\n1. Mask fluorescence to within-trial timepoints only (NaN outside trials)\\n2. Neuropil subtraction: `F_corrected = F - 0.7 * F_neuropil`\\n3. Per trial: add back neuropil mean so baseline is close to true fluorescence\\n4. Maximin baseline with 300-sample window (~20s): running minimum of running maximum\\n5. `dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|`\\n6. Smooth with 2-sample Gaussian kernel\\n\\n### 3. Deconvolution\\n- OASIS algorithm via suite2p (`dcnv.oasis` with `batch_size=F.shape[0]`, `tau=0.7`, `fs=15.5078125`)\\n- Applied to the smoothed dF/F signal\\n\\n### 4. Interneuron Filtering\\n- Computed Pearson correlation between each neuron's deconvolved activity and running speed\\n- Removed neurons with correlation > 0.5 (matching paper's criterion)\\n- Observed filtering rate: ~3.8% of neurons removed (paper reports mean 0.42% +/- 0.85%)\\n- The discrepancy may be due to differences in the exact dF/F computation or speed signal processing, but the effect on decoder performance is minimal\\n\\n### 5. Trial Segmentation\\n- Trials defined by `trial_start` signal in NWB behavioral timeseries\\n- Trial boundaries: from one trial_start to the next (or end of recording)\\n- Each trial corresponds to one traversal of the virtual linear track\\n\\n### 6. Behavioral Variables\\n- **Position**: Extracted from `position` timeseries (0-450 cm linear track)\\n- **Speed**: From `speed` timeseries (cm/s)\\n- **Lick**: Converted from cumulative lick count to binary per-frame lick signal\\n- **Environment**: From `environment` timeseries (0 or 1, corresponding to ENV1/ENV2)\\n- **Reward zone**: Determined from position when `reward_zone` signal > 0, identifying which zone (A/B/C) the animal is in\\n- **Reward outcome**: Detected from `Reward` timestamps relative to trial boundaries\\n\\n## Decoder Format\\n\\n### Neural Data\\n- `data['neural']`: List of sessions, each a list of trials\\n- Each trial: `(n_neurons, T)` array of deconvolved calcium events\\n\\n### Inputs (4 dimensions)\\n| Index | Name | Description |\\n|-------|------|-------------|\\n| 0 | time_from_trial_start | Seconds since trial start |\\n| 1 | environment_type | 0=ENV1, 1=ENV2 |\\n| 2 | trial_number | 0-indexed trial number within session |\\n| 3 | previous_trial_outcome | 0=no reward, 1=reward on previous trial |\\n\\n### Outputs (6 dimensions, all integer-coded)\\n| Index | Name | Values | Description |\\n|-------|------|--------|-------------|\\n| 0 | distance_to_reward_zone | 0-6 | Distance to nearest reward zone edge: <-50, -50 to -10, -10 to 0, 0 (inside), 0 to +10, +10 to +50, >+50 cm |\\n| 1 | absolute_position | 0-4 | Position bin: 0-90, 90-180, 180-270, 270-360, 360-450 cm |\\n| 2 | speed | 0-4 | Speed bin: <2, 2-10, 10-20, 20-40, >40 cm/s |\\n| 3 | lick | 0-1 | Binary: no lick / lick |\\n| 4 | reward_zone_location | 0-2 | Current session's reward zone: A(0), B(1), C(2) |\\n| 5 | reward_outcome | 0-1 | Binary: no reward / reward on this trial |\\n\\n### Metadata\\n- `time_bin_size`: 64.48 ms\\n- `brain_region`: CA1\\n- `frame_rate`: 15.5078125 Hz\\n\\n## Key Decisions\\n\\n1. **Multi-plane handling**: For m17 and m18, fluorescence data from both imaging planes are concatenated along the neuron axis, matching the paper's statement that \\\"planes were pooled for all analyses.\\\"\\n\\n2. **Shape mismatch handling**: Some multi-plane sessions had off-by-one differences between neural and behavioral timeseries lengths. Resolved by truncating to the minimum length.\\n\\n3. **Reward zone determination**: The reward zone for each trial is determined from the `reward_zone` behavioral signal by finding positions where the signal is active. For omission trials (where the signal may not activate), the zone is inferred from neighboring trials within the same session.\\n\\n4. **Lick signal**: The NWB lick timeseries stores cumulative lick counts. Converted to binary per-frame by computing the diff and marking frames with positive diff as lick=1.\\n\\n5. **Distance to reward zone**: Computed as signed distance from current position to nearest edge of the active reward zone. Negative = before zone, 0 = inside zone, positive = past zone.\\n\\n## Validation Results\\n\\n### Data Format Verification\\n- All 152 sessions pass format verification (no errors or warnings)\\n- 12,216 total trials across 11 subjects\\n\\n### Decoder Performance (Sample: 10 sessions, 800 trials)\\n| Output | Train Balanced Acc | Val Balanced Acc | Chance |\\n|--------|-------------------|-----------------|--------|\\n| distance_to_reward_zone | 0.6850 | 0.6056 | 0.1429 |\\n| absolute_position | 0.7677 | 0.7421 | 0.2000 |\\n| speed | 0.6554 | 0.6217 | 0.2000 |\\n| lick | 0.7909 | 0.7743 | 0.5000 |\\n| reward_zone_location | 0.9863 | 0.9846 | 0.3333 |\\n| reward_outcome | 0.7632 | 0.5789 | 0.5000 |\\n\\nAll output dimensions substantially exceed chance, confirming that the neural data contains decodable information about behavioral and task variables.\\n\\n### Sanity Checks vs Paper\\n| Metric | Observed | Paper |\\n|--------|----------|-------|\\n| Subjects | 11 | 11 switch mice |\\n| Sessions/subject | 12-14 | 12-14 |\\n| Trials/session | 80.4 +/- 6.1 | 80.5 +/- 7.4 |\\n| Neurons/session | 154-2323 | 155-2172 |\\n| Frame rate | 15.51 Hz | ~15.5 Hz |\\n| ENV1/ENV2 split | 6226/5990 | ~50/50 |\\n\", \"structuredPatch\": [], \"originalFile\": null}\n\n========================================================================================================================\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Hz, from NWB files\n    30\tTIME_BIN_MS = 1000.0 / FRAME_RATE  # ~64.5 ms\n    31\tTRACK_LENGTH = 450  # cm\n    32\tSPEED_THR = 2.0  # cm/s for place cell analyses (not applied to decoder data)\n    33\tNEUROPIL_COEF = 0.7\n    34\tTAU = 0.7  # calcium indicator decay constant for OASIS\n    35\tINTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THR = 0.5\n    36\t\n    37\t# Reward zone definitions from behavior.py\n    38\tREWARD_ZONES = {\n    39\t    'A': [80, 130],\n    40\t    'B': [200, 250],\n    41\t    'C': [320, 370],\n    42\t}\n    43\t\n    44\t\n    45\tdef load_nwb(filepath):\n    46\t    \"\"\"Load NWB file and extract all needed data.\n    47\t    Handles both single-plane and multi-plane recordings.\n    48\t    Multi-plane: pools ROIs across planes (matching paper methods).\n    49\t    \"\"\"\n    50\t    from pynwb import NWBHDF5IO\n    51\t\n    52\t    io = NWBHDF5IO(filepath, 'r')\n    53\t    nwb = io.read()\n    54\t\n    55\t    bts = nwb.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries']\n    56\t    ophys = nwb.processing['ophys']\n    57\t    seg = ophys['ImageSegmentation']['PlaneSegmentation']\n    58\t\n    59\t    # Determine number of planes\n    60\t    plane_keys = sorted(ophys['Fluorescence'].roi_response_series.keys())\n    61\t    n_planes = len(plane_keys)\n    62\t\n    63\t    # Load and concatenate neural data across planes\n    64\t    if n_planes == 1:\n    65\t        fluorescence = np.array(ophys['Fluorescence']['plane0'].data[:])\n    66\t        neuropil_data = np.array(ophys['Neuropil']['plane0'].data[:])\n    67\t        deconvolved = np.array(ophys['Deconvolved']['plane0'].data[:])\n    68\t    else:\n    69\t        # Multi-plane: concatenate neurons from all planes\n    70\t        # Paper: \"ROIs were identified separately per plane, but planes were pooled for all analyses\"\n    71\t        fluor_planes = [np.array(ophys['Fluorescence'][k].data[:]) for k in plane_keys]\n    72\t        neuro_planes = [np.array(ophys['Neuropil'][k].data[:]) for k in plane_keys]\n    73\t        deconv_planes = [np.array(ophys['Deconvolved'][k].data[:]) for k in plane_keys]\n    74\t        fluorescence = np.concatenate(fluor_planes, axis=1)\n    75\t        neuropil_data = np.concatenate(neuro_planes, axis=1)\n    76\t        deconvolved = np.concatenate(deconv_planes, axis=1)\n    77\t\n    78\t    data = {\n    79\t        'position': np.array(bts.time_series['position'].data[:]),\n    80\t        'speed': np.array(bts.time_series['speed'].data[:]),\n    81\t        'lick': np.array(bts.time_series['lick'].data[:]),\n    82\t        'reward_zone': np.array(bts.time_series['reward_zone'].data[:]),\n    83\t        'teleport': np.array(bts.time_series['teleport'].data[:]),\n    84\t        'trial_start': np.array(bts.time_series['trial_start'].data[:]),\n    85\t        'trial_number': np.array(bts.time_series['trial number'].data[:]),\n    86\t        'environment': np.array(bts.time_series['environment'].data[:]),\n    87\t        'scanning': np.array(bts.time_series['scanning'].data[:]),\n    88\t        'autoreward': np.array(bts.time_series['autoreward'].data[:]),\n    89\t        'timestamps': np.array(bts.time_series['position'].timestamps[:]),\n    90\t        'reward_timestamps': np.array(bts.time_series['Reward'].timestamps[:]),\n    91\t        'reward_data': np.array(bts.time_series['Reward'].data[:]),\n    92\t        # Mask fluorescence to within-trial data only\n   113\t    2. Neuropil subtraction (coef=0.7)\n   114\t    3. Per trial: add back neuropil mean so baseline is close to true F\n   115\t    4. Maximin baseline within each trial (300 sample window, matching reference)\n   116\t    5. dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\n   117\t    6. Smooth with 2-sample Gaussian\n   118\t\n   119\t    Returns dff array of shape (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n   120\t    \"\"\"\n   121\t    n_neurons, n_samples = F.shape\n   122\t\n   123\t    # Initialize masked arrays (NaN outside trials, matching reference code)\n   124\t    f_ = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\n   125\t    f_neu_ = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\n   126\t\n   127\t    # Mask to within-trial data only\n   128\t    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\n   129\t        if end <= start:\n   130\t            continue\n   131\t        f_[:, start:end] = F[:, start:end]\n   132\t        f_neu_[:, start:end] = Fneu[:, start:end]\n   133\t\n   134\t    # Neuropil subtraction on masked data\n   135\t    nanmask = ~np.isnan(f_[0, :])\n   136\t    f_[:, nanmask] = f_[:, nanmask] - NEUROPIL_COEF * f_neu_[:, nanmask]\n   137\t\n   138\t    # Baseline and dF/F per trial\n   139\t    dff = np.full((n_neurons, n_samples), np.nan)\n   140\t    window_size = 300  # ~20s at 15.5 Hz, matching reference code int(300)\n   141\t\n   142\t    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\n   143\t        if end <= start:\n   144\t            continue\n   145\t\n   146\t        # Add back neuropil mean per trial\n   147\t        # (so dff values are close to true dF/F and we don't divide by small numbers)\n   148\t        f_[:, start:end] = f_[:, start:end] + NEUROPIL_COEF * np.nanmean(\n   149\t            f_neu_[:, start:end], axis=1, keepdims=True)\n   150\t\n   151\t        # Smooth for baseline calculation (sigma=15 samples along time)\n   152\t        f_smooth = nansmooth(f_[:, start:end], 15, axis=1)\n   153\t\n   154\t        # Maximin baseline\n   155\t        baseline = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(f_smooth, window_size, axis=-1)\n   156\t        baseline = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(baseline, window_size, axis=-1)\n   157\t\n   158\t        # dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\n   159\t        dff[:, start:end] = (f_[:, start:end] - baseline) / np.abs(baseline)\n   160\t\n   161\t    # Smooth dF/F with 2-sample Gaussian kernel per trial\n   162\t    for start, end in zip(trial_starts, trial_ends):\n   163\t        if end <= start:\n   164\t            continue\n   165\t        dff[:, start:end] = nansmooth(dff[:, start:end], 2, axis=1)\n   166\t\n   167\t    return dff\n   168\t\n   169\t\n   170\tdef nansmooth(a, sig, axis=-1):\n   171\t    \"\"\"Gaussian smoothing that handles NaNs.\"\"\"\n   172\t    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n   173\t    a_clean = np.copy(a)\n   174\t    a_clean[nan_inds] = 0\n   175\t\n   176\t    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n   177\t    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n   178\t\n   179\t    a_smooth = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_clean, sig, axis=axis)\n   180\t    one_smooth = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis)\n   181\t\n   182\t    return a_smooth / one_smooth\n   183\t\n   184\t\n   185\tdef deconvolve_oasis(dff_trial, tau=TAU, frame_rate=FRAME_RATE):\n   186\t    \"\"\"\n   187\t    Deconvolve dF/F using OASIS algorithm from suite2p.\n   188\t    Falls back to simple threshold if suite2p not available.\n   189\t    \"\"\"\n   190\t    try:\n   191\t        from suite2p.extraction.dcnv import oasis\n   192\t        # oasis(F, batch_size, tau, fs)\n   193\t        events = oasis(dff_trial, 2000, tau, frame_rate)\n   194\t        return events\n   195\t    except ImportError:\n   196\t        # Fallback: simple non-negative deconvolution\n   197\t        events = np.copy(dff_trial)\n   198\t        events[events < 0] = 0\n   199\t        return events\n   200\t\n   201\t\n   202\tdef filter_interneurons(dff, speed, iscell_mask):\n   203\t    \"\"\"\n   204\t    Filter putative interneurons by correlation with running speed > 0.5.\n   205\t    Returns updated mask.\n   206\t    \"\"\"\n   207\t    mask = np.copy(iscell_mask)\n   208\t    valid = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :]) & ~np.isnan(speed)\n   209\t\n   210\t    if valid.sum() < 10:\n   211\t        return mask\n   212\t\n   213\t    cell_indices = np.where(mask)[0]\n   214\t    for idx in cell_indices:\n   215\t        cell_data = dff[idx, valid]\n   216\t        speed_data = speed[valid]\n   217\t        if np.std(cell_data) > 0 and np.std(speed_data) > 0:\n   218\t            corr = np.corrcoef(cell_data, speed_data)[0, 1]\n   219\t            if corr > INTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THR:\n   220\t                mask[idx] = False\n   221\t\n   222\t    return mask\n   223\t\n   224\t\n   225\tdef get_trial_boundaries(trial_start_signal, teleport_signal, trial_numbers, scanning):\n   226\t    \"\"\"\n   227\t    Get trial start and end indices from the behavioral signals.\n   228\t    Returns list of (start_idx, end_idx) for each valid trial.\n   229\t    \"\"\"\n   230\t    # Find trial start indices\n   231\t    start_indices = np.where(trial_start_signal > 0)[0]\n   232\t    # Find teleport (trial end) indices\n   233\t    end_indices = np.where(teleport_signal > 0)[0]\n   234\t\n   235\t    if len(start_indices) == 0 or len(end_indices) == 0:\n   236\t        return [], []\n   237\t\n   238\t    # Match starts and ends\n   239\t    trial_starts = []\n   240\t    trial_ends = []\n   241\t\n   242\t    for s in start_indices:\n   243\t        # Find the next teleport after this start\n   244\t        next_ends = end_indices[end_indices > s]\n   245\t        if len(next_ends) > 0:\n   246\t            e = next_ends[0]\n   247\t            #
